--- Copilot on custom_components/lock_code_manager/providers/zwave_js.py:None ---
`_handle_duplicate_code` assumes the slot key is a string (`slot_key = str(code_slot)`) and then reads/writes `entry.data[CONF_SLOTS][slot_key]`. In this codebase slot keys can be either `int` or `str` (e.g., other logic casts with `int(s)` or uses int/str fallbacks), and `get_entry_data()` may be serving `options` while `entry.data` may be empty (startup/options-flow). As written, this can silently skip affected entries (int keys) or raise `KeyError` (slot exists in `options` but not in `data`). Normalize the slot key the same way as elsewhere (support int+str) and update whichever container currently holds the active config (options vs data) to avoid missing/exception cases.
```suggestion
        for entry in self.hass.config_entries.async_entries(DOMAIN):
            if self.lock.entity_id not in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_LOCKS, []):
                continue
            slots = get_entry_data(entry, CONF_SLOTS, {})
            if not slots or code_slot is None:
                continue

            # Determine the actual key used for this slot in the config (int or str).
            if code_slot in slots:
                slot_key = code_slot
            elif str(code_slot) in slots:
                slot_key = str(code_slot)
            else:
                continue

            # Decide whether to update data or options based on where the slot is stored.
            updated = False
            if isinstance(entry.data.get(CONF_SLOTS), dict) and slot_key in entry.data[CONF_SLOTS]:
                new_data = copy.deepcopy(dict(entry.data))
                new_data[CONF_SLOTS][slot_key][CONF_ENABLED] = False
                self.hass.config_entries.async_update_entry(entry, data=new_data)
                updated = True
            elif isinstance(entry.options.get(CONF_SLOTS), dict) and slot_key in entry.options[CONF_SLOTS]:
                new_options = copy.deepcopy(dict(entry.options))
                new_options[CONF_SLOTS][slot_key][CONF_ENABLED] = False
                self.hass.config_entries.async_update_entry(entry, options=new_options)
                updated = True

            if updated:
                affected_entries.append(entry.title or entry.entry_id)
```

--- Copilot on tests/providers/test_zwave_js.py:None ---
The duplicate-code tests only exercise `CONF_SLOTS` keyed with strings (e.g., `{ "2": ... }`), but slot keys can also be `int` in-memory (config flow coerces keys to int). This means the test suite won’t catch regressions where the handler fails to match/disable slots when keys are ints (or when config is temporarily in `options`). Consider adding coverage for an entry with an int slot key (and ideally one where `options` contains the slot) to ensure the notification handler disables the slot reliably.

--- Copilot on custom_components/lock_code_manager/const.py:None ---
The PR description focuses on handling Z-Wave duplicate-code notifications, but this change set also introduces coordinator exponential backoff behavior (new BACKOFF_* constants and coordinator logic/tests). If this is intentional, please reflect it in the PR description (or split into a separate PR) so reviewers/users understand the additional behavioral change being introduced.

--- Copilot on custom_components/lock_code_manager/providers/zwave_js.py:314 ---
`_set_in_progress_code_slot` is cleared on *any* User Code CC value update for the slot, including `userIdStatus` updates. That doesn’t actually confirm the lock accepted the code (only a `userCode` update would), and it can clear the in-progress slot before the duplicate-code notification arrives, causing the duplicate event to be ignored. Consider only clearing this field when `property_name == LOCK_USERCODE_PROPERTY` (and ideally when the new value is non-empty) so failure/status updates don’t cancel duplicate handling.
```suggestion
            # Clear in-progress tracking only when we get a real userCode update
            # for the slot we were setting and the new value is non-empty. This
            # avoids clearing the in-progress slot on status-only/userIdStatus
            # updates, which can race with duplicate-code notifications.
            if (
                property_name == LOCK_USERCODE_PROPERTY
                and code_slot == self._set_in_progress_code_slot
            ):
                new_value = args.get("newValue")
                # Treat any non-empty value (including "0000") as confirmation
                if isinstance(new_value, str):
                    if new_value:
                        self._set_in_progress_code_slot = None
                elif new_value is not None:
                    self._set_in_progress_code_slot = None
```

--- Copilot on custom_components/lock_code_manager/providers/zwave_js.py:525 ---
The PR description mentions creating a persistent notification that lists the affected config entry(ies) so the user knows where to fix the duplicate PIN. As implemented, the notification/error message only includes the lock entity_id and slot number, and doesn’t mention the LCM config entry title(s). If the intent is to guide users to the right configuration, consider including `entry.title` (and/or entry_id) in the message (or aggregating if multiple entries can apply).

   109→    @functools.cached_property
   110→    def _usercode_cc_version(self) -> int:
   111→        """Return the User Code CC version supported by this node."""
   112→        version = next(
   113→            (
   114→                cc.version
   115→                for cc in self.node.command_classes
   116→                if cc.id == CommandClass.USER_CODE
   117→            ),
   118→            0,
   119→        )
   120→        if version == 0:
   121→            _LOGGER.warning(
   122→                "Lock %s: User Code CC not found on node %s. This may "
   123→                "indicate an incomplete interview. Defaulting to V1 behavior",
   124→                self.lock.entity_id,
   125→                self.node.node_id,
   126→            )
   127→            return 1
   128→        return version

   130→    @property
   131→    def supports_push(self) -> bool:
   132→        """
   133→        Return whether this lock supports push-based updates.
   134→
   135→        Z-Wave JS emits value update events when the cache changes, so we
   136→        subscribe to those instead of polling.

   138→        return True

   140→    @property

   142→        """Return whether this lock supports code slot events."""
   143→        return True

   146→    def connection_check_interval(self) -> timedelta | None:
   147→        """Z-Wave JS exposes config entry state changes, so skip polling."""
   148→        return None
   149→
   150→    def _get_client_state(self) -> tuple[bool, str]:
   151→        """Return whether the Z-Wave JS client is ready and a retry reason."""
   152→        if self.lock_config_entry.state != ConfigEntryState.LOADED:
   153→            return False, "config entry not loaded"
   154→
   155→        runtime_data: ZwaveJSData | None = getattr(
   156→            self.lock_config_entry, "runtime_data", None
   157→        )
   158→        client: Client | None = (
   159→            getattr(runtime_data, "client", None) if runtime_data else None
   160→        )
   161→        if not client:
   162→            return False, "Z-Wave JS client not ready"
   163→
   164→        if not client.connected:
   165→            return False, "Z-Wave JS client not connected"
   166→
   167→        if client.driver is None:
   168→            return False, "Z-Wave JS driver not ready"
   169→
   170→        return True, ""

   172→    def code_slot_in_use(self, code_slot: int) -> bool | None:
   173→        """Return whether a code slot is in use."""
   174→        try:
   175→            return get_usercode(self.node, code_slot)[ATTR_IN_USE]
   176→        except (KeyError, ValueError):
   177→            return None
   178→
   179→    def _get_slot_entity_states(self, code_slot: int) -> tuple[str, str, str] | None:
   180→        """
   181→        Get active state, PIN value, and PIN entity ID for a managed slot.
   182→
   183→        Returns tuple of (active_state, pin_value, pin_entity_id) if the slot is
   184→        managed by LCM and entities exist, None otherwise.
   185→        """
   186→        try:
   187→            config_entry = next(
   188→                entry
   189→                for entry in self.hass.config_entries.async_entries(DOMAIN)
   190→                if self.lock.entity_id in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_LOCKS, [])
   191→                and code_slot in (int(s) for s in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_SLOTS, {}))
   192→            )
   193→        except StopIteration:
   194→            return None
   195→
   196→        base_unique_id = f"{config_entry.entry_id}|{code_slot}"
   197→        active_entity_id = self.ent_reg.async_get_entity_id(
   198→            BINARY_SENSOR_DOMAIN, DOMAIN, f"{base_unique_id}|{ATTR_ACTIVE}"
   199→        )
   200→        pin_entity_id = self.ent_reg.async_get_entity_id(
   201→            TEXT_DOMAIN, DOMAIN, f"{base_unique_id}|{CONF_PIN}"
   202→        )
   203→
   204→        if not active_entity_id or not pin_entity_id:
   205→            return None
   206→
   207→        active_state = self.hass.states.get(active_entity_id)
   208→        pin_state = self.hass.states.get(pin_entity_id)
   209→
   210→        if not active_state or not pin_state:
   211→            return None
   212→
   213→        return (active_state.state, pin_state.state, pin_entity_id)
   214→
   215→    def _slot_expects_pin(self, code_slot: int) -> bool:
   216→        """
   217→        Check if LCM expects a PIN on this slot (active=ON with PIN set).
   218→
   219→        Used to ignore stale userIdStatus=AVAILABLE events from locks that report
   220→        old status after a code was successfully set.
   221→        """
   222→        states = self._get_slot_entity_states(code_slot)
   223→        if states is None:
   224→            return False
   225→
   226→        active_state, pin_value, _ = states
   227→        return active_state == STATE_ON and bool(pin_value)
   228→
   229→    def _resolve_pin_if_masked(self, value: str, code_slot: int) -> str | None:
   230→        """
   231→        Resolve a PIN value, looking up expected PIN if masked.
   232→
   233→        Some locks return masked values (all asterisks) instead of the actual PIN.
   234→        This method returns the value as-is if not masked, or looks up the expected
   235→        PIN from LCM entities if masked.
   236→
   237→        """
   238→        slot_in_use = self.code_slot_in_use(code_slot)
   239→
   240→        if value == "0" * len(value) and slot_in_use is False:
   241→            # Some locks return all zeros instead of a blank value when cleared - treat
   242→            # as unmasked cleared value
   243→            return ""
   244→
   245→        # If not masked, return as-is
   246→        if not value or not (value == "*" * len(value) and slot_in_use):
   247→            return value
   248→
   249→        # Masked - look up expected PIN from LCM entities
   250→        # Use ATTR_ACTIVE binary sensor (not CONF_ENABLED switch) to match what
   251→        # sync logic expects. ATTR_ACTIVE considers conditions, so we only resolve
   252→        # masked codes when sync expects a PIN on the lock.
   253→        states = self._get_slot_entity_states(code_slot)
   254→        if states is None:
   255→            return None
   256→
   257→        active_state, pin_value, pin_entity_id = states
   258→
   259→        if active_state == STATE_ON:
   260→            _LOGGER.debug(
   261→                "PIN is masked for lock %s code slot %s, assuming value from PIN entity %s",
   262→                self.lock.entity_id,
   263→                code_slot,
   264→                pin_entity_id,
   265→            )
   266→            return pin_value
   267→
   268→        # Fall back to returning masked value if active state is not ON (e.g. slot not
   269→        # enabled) - we don't care what the value is, just that there is one so that
   270→        # the sync logic treats this slot as having a PIN set on the lock
   271→        return value
   272→
   273→    @callback
   274→    def subscribe_push_updates(self) -> None:
   275→        """Subscribe to User Code CC value update events."""
   276→        # Idempotent - skip if already subscribed
   277→        if self._value_update_unsub is not None:
   278→            return
   279→
   280→        ready, reason = self._get_client_state()
   281→        if not ready:
   282→            _LOGGER.debug(
   283→                "Lock %s: push subscription deferred (%s)",
   284→                self.lock.entity_id,
   285→                reason,
   286→            )
   287→            self._schedule_push_retry(reason)
   288→            return
   289→
   290→        @callback
   291→        def on_value_updated(event: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   292→            """Handle value update events from Z-Wave JS."""
   293→            args: dict[str, Any] = event["args"]
   294→            # Filter for User Code CC - handle both userCode and userIdStatus
   295→            if args.get("commandClass") != CommandClass.USER_CODE:
   296→                return
   297→
   298→            property_name = args.get("property")
   299→            if property_name not in (
   300→                LOCK_USERCODE_PROPERTY,
   301→                LOCK_USERCODE_STATUS_PROPERTY,
   302→            ):
   303→                return
   304→
   305→            code_slot = int(args["propertyKey"])
   306→
   307→            # Slot 0 is not a valid user code slot (used for status/metadata)
   308→            if code_slot == 0:
   309→                return
   310→
   311→            # Clear in-progress tracking when we get a real push update for the
   312→            # slot we were setting — this confirms the lock accepted the code
   313→            if code_slot == self._set_in_progress_code_slot:
   314→                self._set_in_progress_code_slot = None
   315→
   316→            # Handle userIdStatus updates - slot cleared is a fast path
   317→            if property_name == LOCK_USERCODE_STATUS_PROPERTY:
   318→                status = args.get("newValue")
   319→                if status == CodeSlotStatus.AVAILABLE:
   320→                    # Ignore AVAILABLE status if LCM expects a PIN on this slot.
   321→                    # Some locks send stale AVAILABLE events after a code was set,
   322→                    # which would cause infinite sync loops.
   323→                    if self._slot_expects_pin(code_slot):
   324→                        _LOGGER.debug(
   325→                            "Lock %s: ignoring userIdStatus=AVAILABLE for slot %s "
   326→                            "(LCM expects PIN on this slot)",
   327→                            self.lock.entity_id,
   328→                            code_slot,
   329→                        )
   330→                        return
   331→
   332→                    # Slot was cleared - update coordinator if needed
   333→                    if self.coordinator and self.coordinator.data.get(code_slot) != "":
   334→                        _LOGGER.debug(
   335→                            "Lock %s: slot %s userIdStatus=AVAILABLE, marking cleared",
   336→                            self.lock.entity_id,
   337→                            code_slot,
   338→                        )
   339→                        self.coordinator.push_update({code_slot: ""})
   340→                return
   341→
   342→            # Handle userCode updates
   343→            # newValue is the raw PIN string (or None/empty/"0000" if cleared)
   344→            new_value = args.get("newValue")
   345→            # Treat empty, None, or all-zeros as cleared
   346→            if not new_value or (
   347→                isinstance(new_value, str) and new_value.strip("0") == ""
   348→            ):
   349→                value = ""
   350→            else:
   351→                value = str(new_value)
   352→
   353→            # Resolve masked codes (all asterisks) to expected PIN
   354→            # This prevents infinite sync loops when locks return masked values
   355→            resolved = self._resolve_pin_if_masked(value, code_slot)
   356→            if resolved is None:
   357→                _LOGGER.debug(
   358→                    "Lock %s: skipping masked code update for slot %s "
   359→                    "(unable to resolve)",
   360→                    self.lock.entity_id,
   361→                    code_slot,
   362→                )
   363→                return
   364→
   365→            # Skip if value hasn't changed (Z-Wave JS sends duplicate events)
   366→            if self.coordinator and self.coordinator.data.get(code_slot) == resolved:
   367→                return
   368→
   369→            _LOGGER.debug(
   370→                "Lock %s received push update for slot %s: %s",
   371→                self.lock.entity_id,
   372→                code_slot,
   373→                "****" if resolved else "(cleared)",
   374→            )
   375→
   376→            # Push update to coordinator
   377→            if self.coordinator:
   378→                self.coordinator.push_update({code_slot: resolved})
   379→
   380→        try:
   381→            self._value_update_unsub = self.node.on("value updated", on_value_updated)
   382→        except ValueError as err:
   383→            _LOGGER.debug(
   384→                "Lock %s push subscription deferred: %s", self.lock.entity_id, err
   385→            )
   386→            self._schedule_push_retry("node not ready")
   387→
   388→    def _schedule_push_retry(self, reason: str) -> None:
   389→        """Schedule a retry for push subscription if one isn't pending."""
   390→        if self._push_retry_cancel:
   391→            return
   392→
   393→        _LOGGER.debug(
   394→            "Lock %s: scheduling push subscription retry in %ss (%s)",
   395→            self.lock.entity_id,
   396→            PUSH_SUBSCRIBE_RETRY_DELAY,
   397→            reason,
   398→        )
   399→        self._push_retry_cancel = async_call_later(
   400→            self.hass, PUSH_SUBSCRIBE_RETRY_DELAY, self._handle_push_retry
   401→        )
   402→
   403→    @callback
   404→    def _handle_push_retry(self, _now: Any) -> None:
   405→        """Retry push subscription after delay."""
   406→        if self._push_retry_cancel:
   407→            self._push_retry_cancel()
   408→            self._push_retry_cancel = None
   409→        self.subscribe_push_updates()
   410→
   411→    @callback
   412→    def unsubscribe_push_updates(self) -> None:
   413→        """Unsubscribe from value update events."""
   414→        if self._push_retry_cancel:
   415→            self._push_retry_cancel()
   416→            self._push_retry_cancel = None
   417→        if self._value_update_unsub:
   418→            self._value_update_unsub()
   419→            self._value_update_unsub = None
   420→
   421→    @callback
   422→    def _zwave_js_event_filter(self, event_data: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
   423→        """Filter out events."""
   424→        # Try to find the lock that we are getting an event for, skipping
   425→        # ones that don't match
   426→        assert self.node.client.driver
   427→        return (
   428→            event_data[ATTR_HOME_ID] == self.node.client.driver.controller.home_id
   429→            and event_data[ATTR_NODE_ID] == self.node.node_id
   430→            and event_data[ATTR_DEVICE_ID] == self.lock.device_id
   431→        )
   432→
   433→    @callback
   434→    def _handle_zwave_js_event(self, evt: Event) -> None:
   435→        """Handle Z-Wave JS event."""
   436→        if evt.data[ATTR_TYPE] != NotificationType.ACCESS_CONTROL:
   437→            _LOGGER.debug(
   438→                "Lock %s received non Access Control event: %s",
   439→                self.lock.entity_id,
   440→                evt.as_dict(),
   441→            )
   442→            return
   443→
   444→        params = evt.data.get(ATTR_PARAMETERS) or {}
   445→        code_slot = params.get("userId", 0)
   446→
   447→        # Handle duplicate code rejection — only when LCM initiated the set
   448→        if (
   449→            evt.data[ATTR_EVENT]
   450→            == AccessControlNotificationEvent.NEW_USER_CODE_NOT_ADDED_DUE_TO_DUPLICATE_CODE
   451→            and self._set_in_progress_code_slot is not None
   452→            and code_slot in (0, self._set_in_progress_code_slot)
   453→        ):
   454→            self.hass.async_create_task(
   455→                self._async_handle_duplicate_code(),
   456→                f"Handle duplicate code for {self.lock.entity_id} slot {code_slot}",
   457→            )
   458→            return
   459→
   460→        self.async_fire_code_slot_event(
   461→            code_slot=code_slot,
   462→            to_locked=next(
   463→                (
   464→                    to_locked
   465→                    for to_locked, codes in ACCESS_CONTROL_NOTIFICATION_TO_LOCKED.items()
   466→                    if evt.data[ATTR_EVENT] in codes
   467→                ),
   468→                None,
   469→            ),
   470→            action_text=evt.data.get(ATTR_EVENT_LABEL),
   471→            source_data=evt,
   472→        )
   473→
   474→    async def _async_handle_duplicate_code(self) -> None:
   475→        """Handle duplicate code rejection from the lock."""
   476→        lock_ent_id = self.lock.entity_id
   477→        code_slot = self._set_in_progress_code_slot
   478→        self._set_in_progress_code_slot = None
   479→        if code_slot is None:
   480→            return
   481→        try:
   482→            # Only move forward if a config entry manages this lock and code slot - there
   483→            # can only be one due to our config entry uniqueness requirement.
   484→            entry = next(
   485→                entry
   486→                for entry in self.hass.config_entries.async_entries(DOMAIN)
   487→                if self.lock.entity_id in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_LOCKS, [])
   488→                and code_slot in (int(s) for s in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_SLOTS, {}))
   489→            )
   490→        except StopIteration:
   491→            return
   492→
   493→        # Guard that will never trigger but satisfies type checkers that an entity exists
   494→        if (
   495→            enabled_ent_id := self.ent_reg.async_get_entity_id(
   496→                SWITCH_DOMAIN, DOMAIN, f"{entry.entry_id}|{code_slot}|{CONF_ENABLED}"
   497→            )
   498→        ) is None:
   499→            return
   500→
   501→        await self.hass.services.async_call(
   502→            SWITCH_DOMAIN,
   503→            SERVICE_TURN_OFF,
   504→            {ATTR_ENTITY_ID: enabled_ent_id},
   505→            blocking=True,
   506→        )
   507→
   508→        def _dupe_msg(lock_entity_id: str, code_slot: str) -> str:
   509→            """Return message for duplicate code rejection."""
   510→            return (
   511→                f"Lock {lock_entity_id} rejected the code for slot {code_slot} because "
   512→                f"it duplicates a code in another slot. Slot {code_slot} has been "
   513→                f"disabled. Please set a unique code and re-enable slot {code_slot}"
   514→            )
   515→
   516→        _LOGGER.error(
   517→            _dupe_msg("%s", "%s"), lock_ent_id, code_slot, code_slot, code_slot
   518→        )
   519→
   520→        async_create(
   521→            self.hass,
   522→            _dupe_msg(f"**{lock_ent_id}**", f"**{code_slot}**"),
   523→            title="Duplicate Lock Code Rejected",
   524→            notification_id=(f"{DOMAIN}_{lock_ent_id}_{code_slot}_dupe"),
   525→        )
   526→
   527→    @property
   528→    def domain(self) -> str:
   529→        """Return integration domain."""
   530→        return ZWAVE_JS_DOMAIN
   531→
   532→    async def async_setup(self, config_entry: ConfigEntry) -> None:
   533→        """Set up lock."""
   534→        await super().async_setup(config_entry)
   535→        self._listeners.append(
   536→            self.hass.bus.async_listen(
   537→                ZWAVE_JS_NOTIFICATION_EVENT,
   538→                self._handle_zwave_js_event,
   539→                self._zwave_js_event_filter,
   540→            )
   541→        )
   542→
   543→    async def async_unload(self, remove_permanently: bool) -> None:
   544→        """Unload lock."""
   545→        for listener in self._listeners:
   546→            listener()
   547→        self._listeners.clear()
   548→        await super().async_unload(remove_permanently)
   549→
   550→    async def async_is_connection_up(self) -> bool:
   551→        """Return whether connection to lock is up."""
   552→        ready, _reason = self._get_client_state()
   553→        return ready
   554→
   555→    async def async_hard_refresh_codes(self) -> dict[int, int | str]:
   556→        """
   557→        Perform hard refresh and return all codes.
   558→
   559→        Uses Z-Wave JS's refresh_cc_values which handles checksum optimization
   560→        internally - it will skip re-fetching codes if the checksum hasn't changed.
   561→        Returns codes in the same format as async_get_usercodes().
   562→        """
   563→        await self._async_refresh_usercode_cache()
   564→        return await self.async_get_usercodes()
   565→
   566→    async def async_set_usercode(
   567→        self, code_slot: int, usercode: int | str, name: str | None = None
   568→    ) -> bool:
   569→        """
   570→        Set a usercode on a code slot.
   571→
   572→        Returns True if the value was changed, False if already set to this value.
   573→        """
   574→        # Check if the code is already set to this value (avoid unnecessary network call)
   575→        usercode = str(usercode)
   576→        try:
   577→            if (current := get_usercode(self.node, code_slot)).get("in_use"):
   578→                current_code = str(current.get("usercode", ""))
   579→                # Resolve masked codes and compare
   580→                if usercode == self._resolve_pin_if_masked(current_code, code_slot):
   581→                    _LOGGER.debug(
   582→                        "Lock %s slot %s already has this PIN, skipping set",
   583→                        self.lock.entity_id,
   584→                        code_slot,
   585→                    )
   586→                    return False
   587→        except Exception:
   588→            # If we can't check the cache, proceed with the set
   589→            pass
   590→
   591→        self._set_in_progress_code_slot = code_slot
   592→        service_data = {
   593→            ATTR_ENTITY_ID: self.lock.entity_id,
   594→            ATTR_CODE_SLOT: code_slot,
   595→            ATTR_USERCODE: usercode,
   596→        }
   597→        await self.async_call_service(
   598→            ZWAVE_JS_DOMAIN, SERVICE_SET_LOCK_USERCODE, service_data
   599→        )
   600→        # V1 locks don't reliably update the Z-Wave JS value cache after set.
   601→        # Poll the slot directly from the device to force-update the cache
   602→        # before the coordinator reads it, preventing sync loops.
   603→        # No try-except: if the poll fails, we must not proceed with the
   604→        # optimistic update since async_request_refresh() would overwrite it
   605→        # with stale cache data, reintroducing the sync loop. Letting the
   606→        # error propagate allows the sync mechanism to retry the operation.
   607→        if self._usercode_cc_version < 2:
   608→            await get_usercode_from_node(self.node, code_slot)
   609→        # Optimistic update: Z-Wave command succeeded (lock acknowledged), but the
   610→        # value cache updates asynchronously via push notification. Update coordinator
   611→        # immediately to prevent sync loops from reading stale cache data.
   612→        if self.coordinator:
   613→            self.coordinator.push_update({code_slot: usercode})
   614→        return True
   615→
   616→    async def async_clear_usercode(self, code_slot: int) -> bool:
   617→        """
   618→        Clear a usercode on a code slot.
   619→
   620→        Returns True if the value was changed, False if already cleared.
   621→        """
   622→        # Check if the slot is already cleared (avoid unnecessary network call)
   623→        try:
   624→            current = get_usercode(self.node, code_slot)
   625→            if not current.get("in_use"):
   626→                _LOGGER.debug(
   627→                    "Lock %s slot %s already cleared, skipping clear",
   628→                    self.lock.entity_id,
   629→                    code_slot,
   630→                )
   631→                return False
   632→        except Exception:
   633→            # If we can't check the cache, proceed with the clear
   634→            pass
   635→
   636→        service_data = {
   637→            ATTR_ENTITY_ID: self.lock.entity_id,
   638→            ATTR_CODE_SLOT: code_slot,
   639→        }
   640→        await self.async_call_service(
   641→            ZWAVE_JS_DOMAIN, SERVICE_CLEAR_LOCK_USERCODE, service_data
   642→        )
   643→        # V1 locks don't reliably update the Z-Wave JS value cache after clear.
   644→        # Poll the slot directly from the device to force-update the cache
   645→        # before the coordinator reads it, preventing sync loops.
   646→        # See comment in async_set_usercode for why this is not wrapped in
   647→        # try-except.
   648→        if self._usercode_cc_version < 2:
   649→            await get_usercode_from_node(self.node, code_slot)
   650→        # Optimistic update: Z-Wave command succeeded (lock acknowledged), but the
   651→        # value cache updates asynchronously via push notification. Update coordinator
   652→        # immediately to prevent sync loops from reading stale cache data.
   653→        if self.coordinator:
   654→            self.coordinator.push_update({code_slot: ""})
   655→        return True
   656→
   657→    def _get_usercodes_from_cache(self) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   658→        """Get usercodes from Z-Wave JS value DB cache."""
   659→        try:
   660→            return list(get_usercodes(self.node) or [])
   661→        except Exception as err:
   662→            raise LockDisconnected from err
   663→
   664→    async def _async_refresh_usercode_cache(self) -> None:
   665→        """Refresh usercode cache from the device."""
   666→        try:
   667→            await self.node.async_refresh_cc_values(CommandClass.USER_CODE)
   668→        except Exception as err:
   669→            raise LockDisconnected from err
   670→
   671→    async def async_get_usercodes(self) -> dict[int, int | str]:
   672→        """Get dictionary of code slots and usercodes."""
   673→        code_slots = {
   674→            int(code_slot)
   675→            for entry in self.hass.config_entries.async_entries(DOMAIN)
   676→            for code_slot in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_SLOTS, {})
   677→            if self.lock.entity_id in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_LOCKS, [])
   678→        }
   679→        data: dict[int, int | str] = {}
   680→
   681→        if not await self.async_is_connection_up():
   682→            raise LockDisconnected
   683→
   684→        slots = self._get_usercodes_from_cache()
   685→        slots_by_num = {int(slot["code_slot"]): slot for slot in slots}
   686→
   687→        # If any configured slot is missing or has unknown state, do one hard
   688→        # refresh to populate the cache. This is more efficient than fetching
   689→        # individual slots and uses Z-Wave JS's checksum optimization.
   690→        # Note: We call _async_refresh_usercode_cache directly here to avoid
   691→        # recursion since async_hard_refresh_codes calls async_get_usercodes.
   692→        if any(
   693→            slot_num not in slots_by_num or slots_by_num[slot_num].get("in_use") is None
   694→            for slot_num in code_slots
   695→        ):
   696→            _LOGGER.debug(
   697→                "Lock %s has missing/unknown slots, performing hard refresh",
   698→                self.lock.entity_id,
   699→            )
   700→            await self._async_refresh_usercode_cache()
   701→            slots = self._get_usercodes_from_cache()
   702→            slots_by_num = {int(slot["code_slot"]): slot for slot in slots}
   703→
   704→        for slot in slots:
   705→            code_slot = int(slot["code_slot"])
   706→            usercode: str = slot["usercode"] or ""
   707→            in_use: bool | None = slot["in_use"]
   708→
   709→            if not in_use:
   710→                data[code_slot] = ""
   711→            # Resolve PIN (handles masked codes), skip if unresolvable
   712→            elif resolved := self._resolve_pin_if_masked(usercode, code_slot):
   713→                data[code_slot] = resolved
   714→
   715→        slots_with_pin = [s for s, v in data.items() if v]
   716→        slots_not_enabled = [s for s, v in data.items() if not v]
   717→        _LOGGER.debug(
   718→            "Lock %s: %s slots with PIN %s, %s slots not enabled %s",
   719→            self.lock.entity_id,
   720→            len(slots_with_pin),
   721→            slots_with_pin,
   722→            len(slots_not_enabled),
   723→            slots_not_enabled,
   724→        )
   725→        return data
   726→
```

> AGENT

Done. All 3 valid Copilot comments addressed:

1. **In-progress clearing** — Now only clears `_set_in_progress_code_slot` on `LOCK_USERCODE_PROPERTY` updates, not `userIdStatus` updates that could race with duplicate notifications
2. **Entry title in notification** — Message now includes `(config entry <title>)` so users know which LCM config to fix
3. **Task name slot** — Uses `_set_in_progress_code_slot` when `userId` is 0

Skipped 3 comments: #1/#2 (slot key type — code already handles via `int(s)` conversion and `get_entry_data`), #3 (backoff changes — don't exist, hallucination).

  2165→    hass: HomeAssistant,

  2182→    switch_3 = _get_enabled_switch_entity_id(hass, lcm_entry.entry_id, 3)

  2190→    lock_schlage_be469.receive_event(
  2191→        _make_duplicate_code_event(lock_schlage_be469.node_id, user_id=3)

  2197→    assert hass.states.get(switch_3).state == STATE_ON
  2198→
  2199→    # In-progress should NOT be cleared (it wasn't our event)
  2200→    assert lock_instance._set_in_progress_code_slot == 2
  2201→
  2202→    await hass.config_entries.async_unload(lcm_entry.entry_id)

   479→    async def _async_handle_duplicate_code(self) -> None:
   480→        """Handle duplicate code rejection from the lock."""
   481→        lock_ent_id = self.lock.entity_id
   482→        code_slot = self._set_in_progress_code_slot
   483→        self._set_in_progress_code_slot = None
   484→        if code_slot is None:
   485→            return
   486→        try:
   487→            # Only move forward if a config entry manages this lock and code slot - there
   488→            # can only be one due to our config entry uniqueness requirement.
   489→            entry = next(
   490→                entry
   491→                for entry in self.hass.config_entries.async_entries(DOMAIN)
   492→                if self.lock.entity_id in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_LOCKS, [])
   493→                and code_slot in (int(s) for s in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_SLOTS, {}))
   494→            )
   495→        except StopIteration:
   496→            return
   497→
   498→        # Guard that will never trigger but satisfies type checkers that an entity exists
   499→        if (
   500→            enabled_ent_id := self.ent_reg.async_get_entity_id(
   501→                SWITCH_DOMAIN, DOMAIN, f"{entry.entry_id}|{code_slot}|{CONF_ENABLED}"
   502→            )
   503→        ) is None:
   504→            return
   505→
   506→        await self.hass.services.async_call(
   507→            SWITCH_DOMAIN,
   508→            SERVICE_TURN_OFF,
   509→            {ATTR_ENTITY_ID: enabled_ent_id},
   510→            blocking=True,
   511→        )
   512→
   513→        entry_title = entry.title or entry.entry_id
   514→
   515→        def _dupe_msg(
   516→            lock_entity_id: str, code_slot: str, config_entry_title: str
   517→        ) -> str:
   518→            """Return message for duplicate code rejection."""
   519→            return (
   520→                f"Lock {lock_entity_id} rejected the code for slot {code_slot} "
   521→                f"(config entry {config_entry_title}) because it duplicates a code "
   522→                f"in another slot. Slot {code_slot} has been disabled. Please set a "
   523→                f"unique code and re-enable slot {code_slot}"
   524→            )
   525→
   526→        _LOGGER.error(
   527→            _dupe_msg("%s", "%s", "%s"),
   528→            lock_ent_id,
   529→            code_slot,
   530→            entry_title,
   531→            code_slot,
   532→            code_slot,
   533→        )
   534→
   535→        async_create(
   536→            self.hass,
   537→            _dupe_msg(f"**{lock_ent_id}**", f"**{code_slot}**", f"**{entry_title}**"),
   538→            title="Duplicate Lock Code Rejected",
   539→            notification_id=(f"{DOMAIN}_{lock_ent_id}_{code_slot}_dupe"),
   540→        )

  1956→@pytest.fixture
  1957→def mock_zwave_usercodes(zwave_client: MagicMock):
  1958→    """Mock Z-Wave JS usercode functions with mutable state.
  1959→
  1960→    Both ``get_usercodes`` and ``get_usercode`` read from a shared mutable
  1961→    ``codes`` dict.  The client's ``async_send_command`` is wrapped so that
  1962→    set / clear Z-Wave commands automatically update ``codes``, preventing
  1963→    the coordinator refresh from overwriting optimistic push updates with
  1964→    stale data.
  1965→
  1966→    Yields ``(mock_get_usercodes, mock_get_usercode, codes)`` where *codes*
  1967→    is ``dict[int, dict]`` keyed by slot number.
  1968→    """
  1969→    codes: dict[int, dict] = {}
  1970→
  1971→    original_side_effect = zwave_client.async_send_command.side_effect
  1972→
  1973→    async def _send_command_with_codes(message, require_schema=None):
  1974→        if message.get("command") == "node.set_value":
  1975→            vid = message.get("valueId", {})
  1976→            if vid.get("commandClass") == CommandClass.USER_CODE:
  1977→                slot = vid.get("propertyKey")
  1978→                if slot is not None:
  1979→                    prop = vid.get("property")
  1980→                    if prop == "userIdStatus":
  1981→                        # Clear operation
  1982→                        codes[slot] = {
  1983→                            "code_slot": slot,
  1984→                            "in_use": False,
  1985→                            "usercode": "",
  1986→                        }
  1987→                    elif prop == "userCode":
  1988→                        # Set operation
  1989→                        codes[slot] = {
  1990→                            "code_slot": slot,
  1991→                            "in_use": True,
  1992→                            "usercode": str(message["value"]),
  1993→                        }
  1994→        return await original_side_effect(message, require_schema)
  1995→
  1996→    with (
  1997→        patch(
  1998→            "custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercodes",
  1999→        ) as mock_all,
  2000→        patch(
  2001→            "custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercode",
  2002→        ) as mock_one,
  2003→    ):
  2004→        mock_all.side_effect = lambda node: list(codes.values())
  2005→        mock_one.side_effect = lambda node, slot: codes.get(
  2006→            slot, {"code_slot": slot, "in_use": False, "usercode": ""}
  2007→        )
  2008→        zwave_client.async_send_command.side_effect = _send_command_with_codes
  2009→        yield mock_all, mock_one, codes
  2010→        zwave_client.async_send_command.side_effect = original_side_effect
  2011→

   406→
   407→    @callback
   408→    def _handle_push_retry(self, _now: Any) -> None:
   409→        """Retry push subscription after delay."""
   410→        if self._push_retry_cancel:
   411→            self._push_retry_cancel()
   412→            self._push_retry_cancel = None
   413→        self.subscribe_push_updates()
   414→
   415→    @callback
   416→    def unsubscribe_push_updates(self) -> None:
   417→        """Unsubscribe from value update events."""
   418→        if self._push_retry_cancel:
   419→            self._push_retry_cancel()
   420→            self._push_retry_cancel = None
   421→        if self._value_update_unsub:
   422→            self._value_update_unsub()
   423→            self._value_update_unsub = None
   424→
   425→    @callback
   426→    def _zwave_js_event_filter(self, event_data: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
   427→        """Filter out events."""
   428→        # Try to find the lock that we are getting an event for, skipping
   429→        # ones that don't match
   430→        assert self.node.client.driver
   431→        return (
   432→            event_data[ATTR_HOME_ID] == self.node.client.driver.controller.home_id
   433→            and event_data[ATTR_NODE_ID] == self.node.node_id
   434→            and event_data[ATTR_DEVICE_ID] == self.lock.device_id
   435→        )

   437→    @callback
   438→    def _handle_zwave_js_event(self, evt: Event) -> None:
   439→        """Handle Z-Wave JS event."""
   440→        if evt.data[ATTR_TYPE] != NotificationType.ACCESS_CONTROL:
   441→            _LOGGER.debug(
   442→                "Lock %s received non Access Control event: %s",
   443→                self.lock.entity_id,
   444→                evt.as_dict(),
   445→            )

   453→            evt.data[ATTR_EVENT]

   462→            )

code_slot is None — simulates race where slot was cleared before task ran\n    lock_instance._set_in_progress_code_slot = None\n    await lock_instance._async_handle_duplicate_code()\n\n    # Switch should still be on, no notification created\n    assert hass.states.get(switch_entity_id).state == STATE_ON\n    notifications = _async_get_or_create_notifications(hass)\n    notification_id = f\"{DOMAIN}_{lock_instance.lock.entity_id}_2_dupe\"\n    assert notification_id not in notifications\n\n    await hass.config_entries.async_unload(lcm_entry.entry_id)\n\n\nasync def test_handle_duplicate_code_early_return_no_matching_entry(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    zwave_integration: MockConfigEntry,\n    lock_entity: er.RegistryEntry,\n    lock_schlage_be469: Node,\n    mock_zwave_usercodes: tuple[MagicMock, MagicMock, dict[int, dict]],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Test _async_handle_duplicate_code returns early when no config entry manages the slot.\"\"\"\n    lcm_entry = await _setup_lcm_entry(\n        hass,\n        lock_entity.entity_id,\n        {\"2\": {CONF_NAME: \"test\", CONF_PIN: \"1234\", CONF_ENABLED: True}},\n        mock_zwave_usercodes,\n    )\n    switch_entity_id = _get_enabled_switch_entity_id(hass, lcm_entry.entry_id, 2)\n    assert hass.states.get(switch_entity_id).state == STATE_ON\n\n    runtime_data: LockCodeManagerConfigEntryData = lcm_entry.runtime_data\n    lock_instance = runtime_data.locks[lock_entity.entity_id]\n\n    # Set in-progress to a slot not managed by any config entry\n    lock_instance._set_in_progress_code_slot = 99\n    await lock_instance._async_handle_duplicate_code()\n\n    # Switch should still be on, no notification created\n    assert hass.states.get(switch_entity_id).state == STATE_ON\n    notifications = _async_get_or_create_notifications(hass)\n    notification_id = f\"{DOMAIN}_{lock_instance.lock.entity_id}_99_dupe\"\n    assert notification_id not in notifications\n    # In-progress should be cleared even on early return\n    assert lock_instance._set_in_progress_code_slot is None\n\n    await hass.config_entries.async_unload(lcm_entry.entry_id)\n\n\nasync def test_handle_duplicate_code_early_return_entity_not_found(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    zwave_integration: MockConfigEntry,\n    lock_entity: er.RegistryEntry,\n    lock_schlage_be469: Node,\n    mock_zwave_usercodes: tuple[MagicMock, MagicMock, dict[int, dict]],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Test _async_handle_duplicate_code returns early when enabled entity is not found.\"\"\"\n    lcm_entry = await _setup_lcm_entry(\n        hass,\n        lock_entity.entity_id,\n        {\"2\": {CONF_NAME: \"test\", CONF_PIN: \"1234\", CONF_ENABLED: True}},\n        mock_zwave_usercodes,\n    )\n    switch_entity_id = _get_enabled_switch_entity_id(hass, lcm_entry.entry_id, 2)\n    assert hass.states.get(switch_entity_id).state == STATE_ON\n\n    runtime_data: LockCodeManagerConfigEntryData = lcm_entry.runtime_data\n    lock_instance = runtime_data.locks[lock_entity.entity_id]\n\n    # Set in-progress to a valid slot but mock entity registry to return None\n    lock_instance._set_in_progress_code_slot = 2\n    with patch.object(lock_instance.ent_reg, \"async_get_entity_id\", return_value=None):\n        await lock_instance._async_handle_duplicate_code()\n\n    # Switch should still be on, no notification created\n    assert hass.states.get(switch_entity_id).state == STATE_ON\n    notifications = _async_get_or_create_notifications(hass)\n    notification_id = f\"{DOMAIN}_{lock_instance.lock.entity_id}_2_dupe\"\n    assert notification_id not in notifications\n    # In-progress should be cleared\n    assert lock_instance._set_in_progress_code_slot is None\n\n    await hass.config_entries.async_unload(lcm_entry.entry_id)\n"

> AGENT

Here's my review:

**The design is clean.** The three-level approach does exactly what you want:

1. `async_setup_internal` (`@final`) — orchestrates: base setup → provider setup → signals completion
2. `_async_setup_internal` — protected base logic (coordinator, push subscription)
3. `async_setup` — overridable hook for providers

**Two things to address:**

1. **`ZWaveJSLock.async_setup` and `VirtualLock.async_setup` removed `super()` calls** — Correct, since coordinator setup moved to `_async_setup_internal`. But the base `async_setup` default calls `self.setup()` (sync hook). For these two providers, `setup()` is a no-op so it doesn't matter, but a provider that overrides both `setup()` and `async_setup` would silently skip `setup()` unless they call `super()`. Worth a brief docstring note on `async_setup` like: "If overriding, call `super().async_setup(config_entry)` to also invoke `setup()`."

2. **Tests pass?** Let me verify since there are a lot of mechanical `async_setup` → `async_setup_internal` renames.

> AGENT

All 259 tests pass. The changes look good — the architecture cleanly separates base infrastructure from provider-specific setup, with `@final` preventing providers from bypassing the coordinator/event machinery. The only suggestion is a docstring tweak on `async_setup` noting the `super()` / `setup()` relationship.

> DEVELOPER

I renamed everything, still have a concern?

> AGENT

No, the `super()` concern is gone. If a provider overrides `async_setup`, they're fully replacing the hook — that's standard and intentional. The sync `setup()` is just a convenience default for providers that don't need async, not something that should implicitly run alongside a custom `async_setup`.

The design is solid. Ready to commit?

There it is. The `setup` method (line 72) was renamed to `setup_provider` in our diff, but it's decorated with `@callback`, not called via executor. The base `async_setup_provider` calls `self.setup_provider()` via `async_add_executor_job`. But `setup_provider` here is a `@callback` — it should run on the event loop, not in an executor.

   294→    @final
   295→    async def async_setup_internal(self, config_entry: ConfigEntry) -> None:
   296→        """
   297→        Set up lock and coordinator, signaling completion to waiters.
   298→
   299→        First run base setup logic, then call provider-specific async_setup_provider()
   300→        for any additional setup steps. This ensures the coordinator is always set up
   301→        and can handle any exceptions raised during provider setup.
   302→        """

   305→            await self.async_setup(config_entry)
   306→        finally:

   309→    async def _async_setup_internal(self, config_entry: ConfigEntry) -> None:
   310→        """Set up lock and coordinator."""
   311→        lock_entity_id = self.lock.entity_id
   312→        # Track the provider's config entry (e.g., zwave_js) so we can resubscribe
   313→        # when that integration reloads or reconnects.
   314→        self._setup_config_entry_state_listener()
   315→

   317→        if self.coordinator is not None:
   318→            self.hass.async_create_task(
   319→                self.coordinator.async_request_refresh(),
   320→                f"Refresh coordinator for {lock_entity_id}",
   321→            )
   322→            return
   323→
   324→        self.coordinator = LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator(self.hass, self, config_entry)
   325→        if config_entry.state == ConfigEntryState.SETUP_IN_PROGRESS:
   326→            try:
   327→                await self.coordinator.async_config_entry_first_refresh()
   328→            except (ConfigEntryNotReady, UpdateFailed) as err:
   329→                LOGGER.warning(
   330→                    "Failed to fetch initial data for lock %s: %s. "
   331→                    "Entities will be created but unavailable until lock is ready.",
   332→                    lock_entity_id,
   333→                    err,
   334→                )
   335→        else:

   337→            if not self.coordinator.last_update_success:

Test passes locally with the committed code. This is likely a flaky timing issue in CI — the `setup_provider` runs in an executor via `async_add_executor_job`, and on a loaded CI runner the coordinator refresh may complete before the mock data is initialized.

Let me check if the test also passes with the current unstaged changes, then commit everything together — that should fix the CI issue since the unstaged renames are the final intended state.

> AGENT

It fails on Python 3.14 only. The issue is timing — `setup()` runs AFTER `_async_setup_internal()` (coordinator creation), but the coordinator needs the mock data during first refresh. Let me verify.

> TOOL

> AGENT

That's the problem. `get_usercodes()` reads from `hass.data[LOCK_DATA]`, which is initialized by `setup()`. But now `_async_setup_internal` (coordinator + first refresh) runs BEFORE `async_setup` (which calls `setup()`). So during the first coordinator refresh, `get_usercodes()` returns `{}` — no codes, no sync needed, zero `set_usercode` calls.

Before this PR, `async_setup` called `setup()` first, then did coordinator creation. The refactor reversed the order.

The fix: `_async_setup_internal` should call `setup()` at the top before coordinator creation, OR `setup()` should be called before `_async_setup_internal` in the wrapper.

> AGENT

The fix is to call `async_setup` (provider hook) BEFORE `_async_setup_internal` (coordinator). Providers need their data initialized before the coordinator does its first refresh.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit

{

  "old_string": "    @final\n    async def async_setup_internal(self, config_entry: ConfigEntry) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Set up lock and coordinator, signaling completion to waiters.\n\n        First run base setup logic, then call provider-specific async_setup_provider()\n        for any additional setup steps. This ensures the coordinator is always set up\n        and can handle any exceptions raised during provider setup.\n        \"\"\"\n        try:\n            await self._async_setup_internal(config_entry)\n            await self.async_setup(config_entry)\n        finally:\n            self._setup_complete.set()",
  "new_string": "    @final\n    async def async_setup_internal(self, config_entry: ConfigEntry) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Set up lock and coordinator, signaling completion to waiters.\n\n        Provider-specific async_setup() runs first so providers can initialize\n        any state the coordinator needs during its first refresh. Then the base\n        setup creates the coordinator and subscribes to push updates.\n        \"\"\"\n        try:\n            await self.async_setup(config_entry)\n            await self._async_setup_internal(config_entry)\n        finally:\n            self._setup_complete.set()"
}

> AGENT

The issue was that the refactor reversed the execution order — `_async_setup_internal` (coordinator + first refresh) ran before `async_setup` (provider hook), but the coordinator's first refresh needs provider-initialized data (like mock codes). Swapped the order so provider setup runs first.

     1→# TODO
     2→
     3→## New Items
     4→
     5→- Unify design across slot and lock data cards, with a preference towards the slot card design.
     6→- Add type checking to CI and pre-commit:
     7→  - Add mypy (or alternative) to pre-commit hooks
     8→  - Add type checking CI job to python-checks.yml
     9→  - Explore alternatives to mypy (Astral may have a replacement - check for "ty" or similar)
    10→  - Fix existing type errors (~30 errors as of Jan 2026)
    11→- Test visual editor for both cards.
    12→
    13→## Testing
    14→
    15→- Strategy UI module has unit tests in `ts/*.test.ts` and Python tests for
    16→  resource registration/unload in `tests/test_init.py`; still need end-to-end
    17→  UI coverage (Lovelace resource registration + reload in a real frontend).
    18→- Add provider tests when new integrations beyond Z-Wave JS and virtual are added.
    19→- Add Z-Wave JS provider tests (requires Z-Wave JS door lock mocks/fixtures).
    20→- Test rate limiting and connection failure timing in live environment.
    21→
    22→## Refactors and Maintenance
    23→
    24→### Simplify Tests
    25→
    26→**Current Issues:**
    27→
    28→- `tests/common.py` has some duplicated mock setup code
    29→- Some tests may have redundant assertions
    30→- Test fixtures could potentially be more reusable across test files
    31→
    32→**Potential Improvements:**
    33→
    34→- Consolidate duplicate test setup code into shared fixtures
    35→- Review test naming conventions for clarity
    36→- Consider parametrized tests where multiple similar tests exist
    37→- Reduce test boilerplate with helper functions
    38→
    39→**Estimated Effort:** Medium (4-8 hours)
    40→**Priority:** Medium
    41→**Status:** Not started
    42→
    43→### Simplify Code
    44→
    45→#### Remove Other Unnecessary Complexity
    46→
    47→**Areas to Review:**
    48→
    49→1. **Dual storage pattern** (`data` + `options` in config entries) - Can this be
    50→   simplified?
    51→2. **Entity unique ID format** - Is `{entry_id}|{slot}|{type}` optimal?
    52→3. **Multiple coordinator instances** - One per lock - could this be unified?
    53→4. **Internal method wrappers** - `async_internal_*` methods with locks - are
    54→   all necessary?
    55→
    56→#### Clarify Config Entry Data vs Options Usage
    57→
    58→Document and standardize when to read from `config_entry.data` vs
    59→`config_entry.options`. Current understanding: prefer `options` only within the
    60→config entry update listener during options updates; elsewhere use `data` to
    61→avoid mid-update inconsistencies. Add helper(s) or guidance to reduce ambiguity
    62→and prevent regressions.
    63→
    64→**Specific Items:**
    65→
    66→- Review if all `_get_entity_state()` calls can be simplified
    67→- Evaluate if `_entity_id_map` dictionary caching is worth the complexity
    68→- Consider if provider `async_internal_*` wrapper methods can be simplified
    69→- Review entity base classes for potential consolidation
    70→
    71→**Estimated Effort:** High (20+ hours)
    72→**Priority:** Low-Medium
    73→**Status:** Not started
    74→
    75→#### Move Sync Logic to Coordinator
    76→
    77→**Current Architecture Issues:**
    78→
    79→- In-sync binary sensor reads PIN config from text entities via
    80→  `_get_entity_state()`
    81→- Reads lock state from coordinator data
    82→- Compares them and triggers sync operations (set_usercode/clear_usercode)
    83→- Cross-entity dependencies create race conditions during startup
    84→- Current guard uses `_attr_is_on is None` to avoid initial sync operations
    85→
    86→**Proposed Solution:**
    87→Move sync logic entirely into the coordinator:
    88→
    89→- Coordinator already has access to both desired state (from config) and actual
    90→  state (from lock)
    91→- Coordinator performs sync operations during its `_async_update_data()` cycle
    92→- Binary sensor becomes read-only, just displays coordinator's computed in-sync
    93→  status
    94→- Text/number/switch entities remain as config views
    95→
    96→**Example Implementation:**
    97→
    98→```python
    99→# In coordinator._async_update_data()
   100→actual_code = await self.provider.get_usercodes()
   101→desired_code = self.config_entry.data[slot]["pin"]
   102→
   103→if actual_code != desired_code and slot_enabled:
   104→    await self.provider.set_usercode(slot, desired_code)
   105→
   106→return {"in_sync": actual_code == desired_code, "actual_code": actual_code}
   107→```
   108→
   109→**Benefits:**
   110→
   111→- Eliminates cross-entity state reading
   112→- Removes `_initial_state_loaded` flag and startup detection logic
   113→- No race conditions during startup
   114→- Simpler, more centralized sync logic
   115→- Coordinator is single source of truth
   116→
   117→**Considerations:**
   118→
   119→- Major architectural change
   120→- Would need to update binary sensor to be read-only
   121→- Config updates still flow through text/switch entities
   122→- Need to ensure coordinator runs sync on config changes
   123→
   124→**Estimated Effort:** High (16-24 hours)
   125→**Priority:** Medium
   126→**Status:** Not started
   127→
   128→### Convert Config and Internal Dicts to Dataclasses
   129→
   130→Convert config entry data to typed dataclasses with `from_dict`/`from_entry`
   131→class methods. Use object instances internally instead of iterating through raw
   132→config dicts. Audit codebase for other complex dicts that would benefit from
   133→dataclass conversion (e.g., slot data, lock state, coordinator data). This
   134→improves type safety, IDE autocompletion, and code readability.
   135→
   136→**Why not Voluptuous?** Voluptuous is for validation, not object instantiation.
   137→Other options like `dacite` or Pydantic add dependencies.
   138→
   139→**Example implementation:**
   140→
   141→```python
   142→@dataclass
   143→class SlotConfig:
   144→    name: str
   145→    pin: str
   146→    enabled: bool = True
   147→    calendar: str | None = None
   148→    number_of_uses: int | None = None
   149→
   150→    @classmethod
   151→    def from_dict(cls, data: dict) -> SlotConfig:
   152→        return cls(
   153→            name=data[CONF_NAME],
   154→            pin=data[CONF_PIN],
   155→            enabled=data.get(CONF_ENABLED, True),
   156→            calendar=data.get(CONF_CALENDAR),
   157→            number_of_uses=data.get(CONF_NUMBER_OF_USES),
   158→        )
   159→
   160→@dataclass
   161→class LCMConfig:
   162→    locks: list[str]
   163→    slots: dict[int, SlotConfig]
   164→
   165→    @classmethod
   166→    def from_entry(cls, entry: ConfigEntry) -> LCMConfig:
   167→        return cls(
   168→            locks=get_entry_data(entry, CONF_LOCKS, []),
   169→            slots={
   170→                int(k): SlotConfig.from_dict(v)
   171→                for k, v in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_SLOTS, {}).items()
   172→            },
   173→        )
   174→```
   175→
   176→**Places to audit for dict-to-dataclass conversion:**
   177→
   178→- `config_entry.data` / `config_entry.options` access patterns
   179→- `get_entry_data()` / `get_slot_data()` return values
   180→- Coordinator `self.data` structure
   181→- Lock provider internal state
   182→
   183→### Handle Disabled Lock Slots
   184→
   185→**Context:** Z-Wave locks have `userIdStatus` which can be `Enabled`, `Available`,
   186→or `Disabled`. Currently the coordinator only stores the code value, not the status.
   187→
   188→**Investigation Needed:**
   189→
   190→1. Test what happens when LCM tries to set a user code on a slot with
   191→   `userIdStatus=Disabled`
   192→2. Determine if we need to explicitly enable the slot before setting a code
   193→3. Check if different lock brands handle disabled slots differently
   194→
   195→**Related:** See "Enhance Coordinator Data Model" below.
   196→
   197→**Estimated Effort:** Medium (4-8 hours)
   198→**Priority:** Medium
   199→**Status:** Not started
   200→
   201→### Enhance Coordinator Data Model with Slot Status
   202→
   203→**Current State:** Coordinator stores `{slot: code}` mapping only.
   204→
   205→**Proposed Change:** Store `{slot: {code, status}}` where status is a generic
   206→LCM enum that providers map to.
   207→
   208→**Generic Status Enum:**
   209→
   210→```python
   211→class SlotStatus(StrEnum):
   212→    ENABLED = "enabled"    # Slot has active code
   213→    AVAILABLE = "available"  # Slot can be used but is empty
   214→    DISABLED = "disabled"  # Slot cannot be used (locked out)
   215→```
   216→
   217→**Provider Mapping:**
   218→
   219→- Z-Wave JS: Maps `userIdStatus` (Enabled/Available/Disabled) → `SlotStatus`
   220→- Other providers: Map their equivalent states to the generic enum
   221→
   222→**Benefits:**
   223→
   224→- Frontend can distinguish between "slot is empty" vs "slot is disabled"
   225→- Better handling of disabled slots in sync logic
   226→- More accurate representation of lock state
   227→- Provider-agnostic data model
   228→
   229→**Implementation:**
   230→
   231→1. Define `SlotStatus` enum in `const.py`
   232→2. Update Z-Wave JS provider to track userIdStatus and map to `SlotStatus`
   233→   (currently filtered out in `on_value_updated`)
   234→3. Change coordinator data schema from `dict[int, str]` to
   235→   `dict[int, SlotData]` where `SlotData` includes code and status
   236→4. Update all consumers of coordinator data (binary_sensor, sensor, websocket)
   237→5. Update frontend types and rendering
   238→
   239→**Estimated Effort:** High (12-16 hours)
   240→**Priority:** Medium
   241→**Status:** Not started
   242→
   243→### Add Optional Flags to `get_config_entry_data` Websocket Command
   244→
   245→**Context:** PR #787 consolidated multiple websocket commands into a single
   246→`get_config_entry_data` command that returns `{config_entry, entities, locks, slots}`.
   247→However, not all callers need all the data:
   248→
   249→| Caller | config_entry | entities | locks | slots |
   250→| ------ | ------------ | -------- | ----- | ----- |
   251→| `view-strategy.ts` | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
   252→| `generate-view.ts` | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |
   253→| `slot-section-strategy.ts` (legacy) | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ |
   254→| `dashboard-strategy.ts` | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ |
   255→| `lock-codes-card-editor.ts` | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ |
   256→
   257→**Cost Analysis:**
   258→
   259→- `config_entry` - Very cheap (just `config_entry.as_json_fragment`)
   260→- `slots` - Very cheap (extracts calendar IDs from config)
   261→- `locks` - Moderate (iterates locks, gets friendly names via state lookups)
   262→- `entities` - Most expensive (queries entity registry, maps to `as_partial_dict`)
   263→
   264→**Proposed Change:** Add optional `include_entities` and `include_locks` flags
   265→(default `True` for backwards compatibility). Skip `config_entry` and `slots`
   266→flags since they're essentially free.
   267→
   268→```python
   269→vol.Optional("include_entities", default=True): bool,
   270→vol.Optional("include_locks", default=True): bool,
   271→```
   272→
   273→**Benefits:**
   274→
   275→- `view-strategy.ts` can skip both entities and locks
   276→- `dashboard-strategy.ts` and `lock-codes-card-editor.ts` can skip entities
   277→- Legacy `slot-section-strategy.ts` can skip locks
   278→
   279→**Estimated Effort:** Low (2-4 hours)
   280→**Priority:** Low
   281→**Status:** Not started
   282→
   283→### Entity Registry Change Detection
   284→
   285→Track entity registry updates and warn if LCM entities change entity IDs (reload
   286→required).
   287→
   288→### Add Provider Diagnostic Data Method
   289→
   290→Add `get_diagnostic_data()` method to `BaseLock` for exposing provider-specific
   291→diagnostic information. Keymaster has this pattern with `get_platform_data()`.
   292→
   293→**Example Implementation:**
   294→
   295→```python
   296→# In BaseLock
   297→def get_diagnostic_data(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
   298→    """Return provider-specific diagnostic data."""
   299→    return {}
   300→
   301→# In ZWaveJSLock
   302→def get_diagnostic_data(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
   303→    return {
   304→        "node_id": self.node.node_id,
   305→        "home_id": self.node.client.driver.controller.home_id,
   306→        "client_connected": self.node.client.connected,
   307→        "config_entry_state": self.lock_config_entry.state.value,
   308→    }
   309→```
   310→
   311→**Benefits:**
   312→
   313→- Easier troubleshooting of provider-specific issues
   314→- Can be exposed via diagnostics platform or websocket
   315→
   316→**Estimated Effort:** Low (2-4 hours)
   317→**Priority:** Low
   318→**Status:** Not started
   319→
   320→### Drift Detection Failure Alerting
   321→
   322→Add mechanism to alert users when drift detection consistently fails over
   323→extended periods (e.g., lock offline). Currently failures are logged but there is
   324→no visibility to users or entities.
   325→
   326→## Features
   327→
   328→### Add Schedule and Entity Condition Types
   329→
   330→**Problem:** Users report that managing calendars in HA is too painful for simple
   331→recurring schedules. Calendars are overkill for "weekdays 9-5" patterns.
   332→
   333→**Proposed Solution:** Add alternative condition types alongside `calendar`:
   334→
   335→| Condition Type | Use Case |
   336→| -------------- | -------- |
   337→| `calendar` | One-time events, external calendar sync (existing) |
   338→| `schedule` | Recurring weekly patterns via HA schedule helper |
   339→| `entity` | Custom logic via any binary sensor/input_boolean |
   340→
   341→**Implementation:**
   342→
   343→1. **Config Flow Changes:**
   344→   - Add condition type selector (calendar/schedule/entity)
   345→   - Show appropriate entity selector based on type
   346→   - `schedule`: EntitySelector filtered to `schedule` domain
   347→   - `entity`: EntitySelector filtered to `binary_sensor`, `input_boolean`
   348→
   349→2. **Binary Sensor Changes:**
   350→   - Update `_get_entity_state()` to handle different condition types
   351→   - Schedule entities: check if current time is within schedule (state = "on")
   352→   - Entity conditions: directly use entity state
   353→
   354→3. **Slot Data Model:**
   355→   - Add `condition_type` field: `calendar | schedule | entity`
   356→   - Add `condition_entity` field (alternative to `calendar`)
   357→   - Maintain backward compatibility with existing `calendar` field
   358→
   359→4. **Frontend Updates:**
   360→   - Update slot card to show condition type
   361→   - Show appropriate icon/label for each type
   362→
   363→**Benefits:**
   364→
   365→- Schedule helper is much simpler for recurring patterns
   366→- Entity condition allows maximum flexibility (templates, automations)
   367→- Calendar remains available for complex/one-time events
   368→
   369→**Estimated Effort:** Medium (8-12 hours)
   370→**Priority:** Medium-High
   371→**Status:** Not started
   372→
   373→### Advanced Calendar Configuration
   374→
   375→**Feature Request:** Allow slot number and PIN to be configured directly in
   376→calendar event metadata, eliminating need for separate slot configuration.
   377→
   378→**Design Considerations:**
   379→
   380→**Current Behavior:**
   381→
   382→- Slot configured in config flow with fixed PIN
   383→- Calendar event (when present) only controls whether slot is active/inactive
   384→- PIN and slot number are static configuration
   385→
   386→**Proposed Behavior:**
   387→
   388→- Calendar event contains slot number and PIN in its metadata
   389→- User configures regex/pattern to extract slot number and PIN from:
   390→  - Event title (e.g., "Slot 3: 1234")
   391→  - Event description
   392→  - Event location
   393→  - Custom calendar properties
   394→
   395→**Implementation Requirements:**
   396→
   397→1. **Config Flow Changes:**
   398→   - Add "advanced calendar mode" toggle per slot
   399→   - When enabled, show pattern configuration UI
   400→   - Pattern fields: slot number regex, PIN regex, which field to parse
   401→     (title/description/location)
   402→2. **Entity Changes:**
   403→   - Calendar event listener needs to parse event metadata
   404→   - Extract slot number and PIN based on user-defined patterns
   405→   - Validate extracted values (numeric slot, PIN format)
   406→3. **Binary Sensor Changes:**
   407→   - Check if calendar event contains valid extracted values
   408→   - Use extracted PIN instead of configured PIN
   409→   - Handle multiple calendar events with different slots
   410→
   411→**Example Patterns:**
   412→
   413→- Title: "Guest Access: Slot 5, PIN 9876" -> Slot: `\d+`, PIN: `\d{4}$`
   414→- Description: "Code: 1234 for slot 3" -> Slot: `slot (\d+)`, PIN: `Code: (\d+)`
   415→- Location: "5:1234" -> Slot: `^(\d+):`, PIN: `:(\d+)$`
   416→
   417→**Challenges:**
   418→
   419→- Multiple calendar events with different slots
   420→- Error handling for invalid patterns
   421→- UI for testing patterns
   422→- Backward compatibility with existing simple calendar mode
   423→
   424→**Estimated Effort:** Very High (40+ hours)
   425→**Priority:** Medium
   426→**Status:** Not started
   427→
   428→### Add Relevant New Home Assistant Core Features
   429→
   430→**Analysis:** Review Home Assistant release notes from 2024.1 through 2025.10
   431→and integrate relevant new features.
   432→
   433→**Key Features to Evaluate (2024-2025):**
   434→
   435→1. **Entity Category Enhancements** (2024.x)
   436→   - New entity categories available
   437→   - **Action:** Review entity category assignments
   438→2. **Selector Improvements** (2024.x)
   439→   - New selector types for config flow
   440→   - **Action:** Review config flow UI for better selectors
   441→3. **Repair Platform** (2024.x)
   442→   - Notify users of configuration issues
   443→   - **Action:** Consider adding repairs for common misconfigurations
   444→
   445→**Already Evaluated (No Changes Needed):**
   446→
   447→- **Config Entry Runtime Data**: `hass.data[DOMAIN]` correctly holds global
   448→  cross-config-entry state (lock registry, resource flag). Per-entry data
   449→  already uses `runtime_data`.
   450→- **DataUpdateCoordinator `_async_setup()`**: All coordinator setup is
   451→  synchronous (timer registration). No async initialization needed.
   452→- **Config Entry Diagnostics**: No significant internal state to expose beyond
   453→  what's already visible via entities and config entries.
   454→
   455→**Estimated Effort:** Low-Medium (4-8 hours)
   456→**Priority:** Medium
   457→**Status:** Partially evaluated
   458→
   459→### Add Support for Additional Lock Providers
   460→
   461→**Current Providers:**
   462→
   463→- Z-Wave JS (`zwave_js.py`)
   464→- Virtual (`virtual.py`) - for testing only
   465→
   466→**Available Lock Integrations in Home Assistant Core:**
   467→
   468→**Smart Home Platforms:**
   469→
   470→- `deconz` - deCONZ (Zigbee/Z-Wave gateway)
   471→- `homematic` - Homematic (CCU)
   472→- `homematicip_cloud` - Homematic IP Cloud
   473→- `homekit_controller` - HomeKit accessories
   474→- `matter` - Matter protocol (PR open)
   475→- `mqtt` - MQTT locks
   476→- `smartthings` - SmartThings
   477→- `zha` - Zigbee Home Automation
   478→- `zwave_js` - Z-Wave JS (already supported)
   479→- `zwave_me` - Z-Wave.Me
   480→
   481→**Brand-Specific Integrations:**
   482→
   483→- `abode` - Abode Security
   484→- `bmw_connected_drive` - BMW Connected Drive
   485→- `dormakaba_dkey` - Dormakaba dkey
   486→- `igloohome` - igloohome
   487→- `kiwi` - Kiwi (Eufy)
   488→- `loqed` - Loqed Smart Lock
   489→- `nuki` - Nuki Smart Lock
   490→- `schlage` - Schlage Encode
   491→- `sesame` - Sesame Smart Lock
   492→- `switchbot` - SwitchBot Lock
   493→- `switchbot_cloud` - SwitchBot Cloud
   494→- `tedee` - Tedee Smart Lock
   495→- `yolink` - YoLink
   496→
   497→**Security Systems:**
   498→
   499→- `simplisafe` - SimpliSafe
   500→- `verisure` - Verisure
   501→
   502→**Cannot Be Supported** (see README for details):
   503→
   504→- `esphome` - No user code API in ESPHome
   505→- `august`, `yale`, `yalexs_ble`, `yale_smart_alarm` - Library limitations
   506→
   507→**Vehicle Integrations:**
   508→
   509→- `subaru` - Subaru Starlink
   510→- `tesla_fleet` - Tesla Fleet API
   511→- `teslemetry` - Teslemetry
   512→- `tessie` - Tessie (Tesla)
   513→- `starline` - StarLine
   514→
   515→**Other Integrations:**
   516→
   517→- `fibaro` - Fibaro
   518→- `freedompro` - Freedompro
   519→- `insteon` - Insteon
   520→- `isy994` - Universal Devices ISY
   521→- `keba` - KEBA EV Charger
   522→- `overkiz` - Overkiz (Somfy TaHoma)
   523→- `surepetcare` - Sure Petcare
   524→- `unifiprotect` - UniFi Protect
   525→- `vera` - Vera
   526→- `wallbox` - Wallbox EV Charger
   527→- `xiaomi_aqara` - Xiaomi Aqara
   528→
   529→**Utility/Helper Integrations:**
   530→
   531→- `demo` - Demo platform
   532→- `group` - Lock groups
   533→- `kitchen_sink` - Testing platform
   534→- `switch_as_x` - Convert switches to locks
   535→- `template` - Template locks
   536→- `homee` - Homee gateway
   537→
   538→**Recommended Priorities for Support:**
   539→
   540→**High Priority** (popular, widely used):
   541→
   542→1. **ZHA (Zigbee Home Automation)** - Very popular, supports many lock brands
   543→2. **Matter** - Future-proof, industry standard (PR open)
   544→3. **MQTT** - Generic protocol, many custom implementations
   545→
   546→**Medium Priority** (brand-specific, popular):
   547→
   548→1. **Nuki** - Popular in Europe
   549→2. **Schlage** - Popular in North America
   550→3. **SwitchBot** - Growing popularity
   551→
   552→**Low Priority** (niche or less common):
   553→
   554→- Vehicle locks (Tesla, BMW, Subaru) - different use case
   555→- Security system locks - usually managed by their own systems
   556→- Utility integrations (template, group) - may work without specific provider
   557→
   558→**Implementation Approach:**
   559→
   560→For each new provider:
   561→
   562→1. Create `providers/INTEGRATION_NAME.py`
   563→2. Subclass `BaseLock`
   564→3. Implement required methods
   565→4. Add integration-specific event listeners
   566→5. Add tests in `tests/INTEGRATION_NAME/test_provider.py`
   567→6. Update documentation
   568→
   569→**Estimated Effort per Provider:** Medium (6-12 hours each)
   570→**Priority:** Medium-High
   571→**Status:** Not started
   572→**Recommended First Addition:** ZHA (Zigbee)
